{"id":"q.knife_why","label":"Why was Straker carrying a small knife?","entry":{"of":"straker","attr":"carrying","time":{"at":"t.straker_leaves"}},"raised":35,"quote":"in his right hand he held a small knife","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":35,"via":"always"},{"value":"for defence, being uneasy after the stranger's visit","available":35,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":35,"text":"He had heard of the stranger and was uneasy when he went out, so he may have armed himself."},"out":221,"by":"f.knife_no_weapon"},{"value":"it was simply his own pocket-knife","available":35,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":35,"text":"A small knife may just be what a man carries every day, drawn when he was attacked."},"out":221,"by":"f.knife_no_weapon"},{"value":"for a delicate task he had in mind","available":82,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":82,"text":"A cataract knife is made for fine cutting, not fighting, and its cork guard lay beside the body: the blade was uncovered with time to spare, not snatched up in a fight."}}],"answer":"for a delicate task he had in mind","answer_given_at":221,"answer_given_quote":"And it was to be used for a delicate operation that night.","answer_inferable_at":175,"answer_reasoning":{"para":175,"text":"A cataract knife is made for fine cutting and its cork guard was off by the body. With the lame sheep, it points to a planned cut rather than self-defence."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.how_many_assailants","label":"Was Straker attacked by one man or by several?","entry":{"of":"?","attr":"action","time":{"at":"t.crime"}},"raised":35,"quote":"defended himself vigorously against his assailants","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":35,"via":"always"},{"value":"more than one","available":35,"via":"direct","quote":"defended himself vigorously against his assailants","out":226,"by":"f.sb_lashes_out"},{"value":"a single man","available":35,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":35,"text":"Nothing in the wounds, the knife or the cravat needs more than one attacker; 'assailants' may be only the reports' way of putting it."},"out":226,"by":"f.sb_lashes_out"},{"value":"none: the horse struck the blow","available":212,"via":"direct","quote":"The real murderer is standing immediately behind you."}],"answer":"none: the horse struck the blow","answer_given_at":212,"answer_given_quote":"The real murderer is standing immediately behind you.","answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.weapon","label":"What struck the blow that shattered Straker's head?","entry":{"of":"straker","attr":"condition","time":{"at":"t.crime"}},"raised":35,"quote":"His head had been shattered by a savage blow from some heavy weapon","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":35,"via":"always"},{"value":"simpson_stick","available":40,"via":"direct","quote":"was just such a weapon as might, by repeated blows","out":226,"by":"f.sb_lashes_out"},{"value":"a kick from the horse","available":37,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":37,"text":"The horse was in the hollow at the time of the struggle, and a horse's kick can shatter a man's skull."}}],"answer":"a kick from the horse","answer_given_at":226,"answer_given_quote":"the steel shoe had struck Straker full on the forehead","answer_inferable_at":212,"answer_from":"f.sb_kills_straker"}
{"id":"q.whose_blood","label":"Whose blood was on Straker's knife?","entry":{"of":"straker_knife","attr":"condition","time":{"at":"t.crime"}},"raised":41,"quote":"one at least of his assailants must bear his mark upon him","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":41,"via":"always"},{"value":"an assailant's","available":41,"via":"direct","quote":"one at least of his assailants must bear his mark upon him","out":226,"by":"f.knife_gashes"},{"value":"Straker's own","available":43,"via":"direct","quote":"may have been caused by his own knife"}],"answer":"Straker's own","answer_given_at":226,"answer_given_quote":"as he fell, his knife gashed his thigh","answer_inferable_at":44,"answer_reasoning":{"para":44,"text":"Simpson has no wound, and Holmes calls it probable that the thigh cut came from Straker's own knife as he fell after the blow."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.cravat_how","label":"How did Simpson's cravat come to be in the dead man's hand?","entry":{"of":"cravat","attr":"location","time":{"at":"t.crime"}},"raised":40,"quote":"utterly unable to account for its presence in the hand of the murdered man","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":40,"via":"always"},{"value":"Straker tore it from Simpson in the struggle","available":40,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":40,"text":"Straker fought his attacker and was found clutching the cravat; if Simpson was the attacker, Straker pulled it off him."},"out":226,"by":"f.straker_picks_cravat"},{"value":"Simpson lost it earlier and someone else had it","available":40,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":40,"text":"Simpson admits being at the stable but cannot account for the cravat; he may have dropped it on the moor that evening, to be picked up by another."}}],"answer":"Simpson lost it earlier and someone else had it","answer_given_at":226,"answer_given_quote":"Simpson in his flight had dropped his cravat, and Straker had picked it up","answer_inferable_at":214,"answer_reasoning":{"para":214,"text":"The horse struck Straker and no man fought him in the hollow, so the cravat was not torn from an attacker. Simpson said he had lost it, and someone picked it up."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.wire_reply","label":"Who answered Holmes's wire to Dartmoor?","kind":"provenance","entry":{"of":"?","attr":"tells","time":{"at":"t.wire_reply"}},"raised":31,"quote":"I sent a special wire to Dartmoor yesterday to clear the matter up","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":31,"via":"always"},{"value":"gregory","available":31,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":31,"text":"Inspector Gregory, in charge of the case, had telegraphed Holmes and would be the natural one to answer."}},{"value":"ross","available":31,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":31,"text":"Colonel Ross, the owner, had also telegraphed Holmes and could have answered."}}],"answer":null,"answer_given_at":null,"answer_given_quote":null,"answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.press_source","label":"Where did the newspapers get the witnesses' accounts?","kind":"provenance","entry":{"of":"?","attr":"tells","time":{"after":"t.tue_hollow","before":"t.wed"}},"raised":12,"quote":"the embellishments of theorists and reporters","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":12,"via":"always"},{"value":"police","available":12,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":12,"text":"The police took the witnesses' statements and could have passed them to the press."}},{"value":"from the witnesses themselves","available":12,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":12,"text":"Reporters on the spot may have spoken to the maid, the lads and Mrs. Straker directly."}}],"answer":null,"answer_given_at":null,"answer_given_quote":null,"answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.why_led_out","label":"Why was the horse led out of the stable instead of being harmed in his stall?","entry":{"of":"?","attr":"intends","time":{"at":"t.horse_leaves_stall"}},"raised":57,"quote":"Why should he take the horse out of the stable? If he wished to injure it why could he not do it there?","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":57,"via":"always"},{"value":"to take him away and hide him","available":57,"via":"indirect","from":"f.police_theory","out":223,"by":"f.straker_wished_out"},{"value":"to take him to the gypsies","available":62,"via":"direct","quote":"might he not have been leading the horse to them when he was overtaken","out":220,"by":"f.straker_takes_horse"},{"value":"to do something to him that could not be done inside the stable","available":57,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":57,"text":"Anyone who only wanted the horse out of the race could have harmed him in the stall. Leading him onto the open moor suggests a purpose that needed him outside."}},{"value":"he was taken out by someone the stable trusted, for a purpose of his own","available":57,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":57,"text":"The horse was bridled and led quietly from a locked stable. That looks less like a raid by an enemy than like a man used to handling him, such as Straker, who went down to the stables at one."}},{"value":"to harm him out on the moor, from which he broke away alive","available":117,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":117,"text":"The horse's own track leads away from the hollow alone and alive, so whoever brought him there did not take him on."}}],"answer":"to do something to him that could not be done inside the stable","also_right":["he was taken out by someone the stable trusted, for a purpose of his own","to harm him out on the moor, from which he broke away alive"],"answer_given_at":223,"answer_given_quote":"It was absolutely necessary to do it in the open air.","answer_inferable_at":175,"answer_reasoning":{"para":175,"text":"A fine cut to the leg, rehearsed on the sheep, would make a spirited horse plunge and wake the lads in the loft. It had to be done out of earshot, by a light hidden in a hollow."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.duplicate_key","label":"Was the stable opened with a duplicate key?","entry":{"of":"duplicate_key","attr":"condition","time":{"after":"t.mon_hunter_runs_out","before":"t.tue_at_stables"}},"raised":57,"quote":"Has a duplicate key been found in his possession?","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":57,"via":"always"},{"value":"yes, and it was thrown away afterwards","available":58,"via":"direct","quote":"The key, having served its purpose, would be hurled away.","out":220,"by":"f.straker_takes_horse"},{"value":"there was no duplicate: the door was opened with a key belonging to the stable","available":57,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":57,"text":"No duplicate key has been found on Simpson, while Straker, master of the stable, would have had his own key and went out to the stables that night."}}],"answer":"there was no duplicate: the door was opened with a key belonging to the stable","answer_given_at":220,"answer_given_quote":"John Straker went down to the stables in the dead of the night and took out Silver Blaze","answer_inferable_at":182,"answer_reasoning":{"para":182,"text":"The silent dog points to a familiar visitor, most plausibly Straker, who had his own key. No duplicate key was ever found."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.opium_source","label":"Where did the powdered opium come from?","entry":{"of":"opium","attr":"location","time":{"at":"t.opium_bought"}},"raised":57,"quote":"What chemist sold him the powdered opium?","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":57,"via":"always"},{"value":"london","available":58,"via":"direct","quote":"The opium was probably brought from London."},{"value":"a chemist in the district","available":57,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":57,"text":"Holmes treats the opium as something that can be traced to a seller. {"id":"q.gypsies_involved","label":"Did the gypsies who camped near the hollow have a part in the affair?","entry":{"of":"gypsy_party","attr":"action","time":{"at":"t.crime"}},"raised":62,"quote":"a party of gypsies encamped on Monday night within a mile of the spot where the murder took place","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":62,"via":"always"},{"value":"they were in league with Simpson","available":62,"via":"direct","quote":"presuming that there was some understanding between Simpson and these gypsies","out":146,"by":"f.brown_sees_horse"},{"value":"they took the horse on their own account","available":62,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":62,"text":"They camped within a mile of the hollow on the night of the murder and were gone the next day; they might have found the horse loose, or taken him."},"out":146,"by":"f.sb_at_mapleton"},{"value":"they had nothing to do with it","available":62,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":62,"text":"A camp near a murder has reason to move on in any case, nothing links them to Simpson, and a large reward has the gypsies of Dartmoor looking for the horse."}}],"answer":"they had nothing to do with it","answer_given_at":114,"answer_given_quote":"And why should gypsies kidnap him?","answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.brown_involved","label":"Have Silas Brown and the Mapleton stables any part in the affair?","entry":{"of":"silas_brown","attr":"action","time":{"at":"t.crime"}},"raised":66,"quote":"that is a factor which we must certainly not neglect","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":66,"via":"always"},{"value":"no part","available":66,"via":"direct","quote":"there is nothing to connect him with the affair","out":146,"by":"f.brown_conceals"},{"value":"a hand in it","available":66,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":66,"text":"Brown has large bets on the race, his horse is second favourite, and he was no friend to Straker. The police search of his stables looked for Silver Blaze as described, and that may not settle it."}},{"value":"he took in the stray horse but had no part in the murder","available":124,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":124,"text":"The horse came alone from the hollow and a man met it near Mapleton, then led it in at the gates. Whoever took it in at Mapleton found a stray and was not the one in the hollow."}}],"answer":"he took in the stray horse but had no part in the murder","also_right":["a hand in it"],"answer_given_at":146,"answer_given_quote":"he perceived a strange horse wandering over the moor","answer_inferable_at":124,"answer_reasoning":{"para":124,"text":"The horse's track leaves the hollow alone; a man's track joins it only near Mapleton, and the return track goes in at the Mapleton gates. Whoever took it in found a stray and was not in the hollow."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.simpson_mapleton","label":"Is Simpson connected with the Mapleton stables?","entry":{"of":"simpson","attr":"condition","time":{"at":"t.crime"}},"raised":67,"quote":"And nothing to connect this man Simpson with the interests of the Mapleton stables?","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":67,"via":"always"},{"value":"no connection","available":68,"via":"direct","quote":"Nothing at all."},{"value":"a connection not yet found","available":67,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":67,"text":"Simpson came down for information on Desborough as well as on the King's Pyland horses, and both he and Mapleton gain if the favourite does not run."}}],"answer":"no connection","answer_given_at":68,"answer_given_quote":"Nothing at all.","answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.holmes_clue","label":"What clue has Holmes found on the drive, and where did he find it?","entry":{"of":"holmes","attr":"knows","time":{"at":"t.thu_holmes_alights"}},"raised":70,"quote":"his hand was upon a clue, though I could not imagine where he had found it","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":70,"via":"always"},{"value":"something in Gregory's talk in the carriage: the gypsies, the stable searches or Mapleton","available":70,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":70,"text":"Holmes fell silent and absorbed straight after Gregory's account of the gypsies, the searches and Mapleton, so one of those points may have set him off."}},{"value":"a point among the facts he listed in the train: the curry, the opium, the dog or the locked door","available":70,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":70,"text":"He has seen no physical evidence yet, so the clue must come from facts already known, and his summary held several odd points."}},{"value":"something in the view from the villa, with Mapleton in sight across the moor","available":70,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":69,"text":"He stared ahead as they pulled up within sight of the out-building and the Mapleton stables, and roused himself only when touched."}}],"answer":"a point among the facts he listed in the train: the curry, the opium, the dog or the locked door","answer_given_at":216,"answer_given_quote":"the immense significance of the curried mutton occurred to me","answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.who_derbyshire","label":"Who is William Derbyshire?","entry":{"of":"derbyshire","attr":"identity","time":{"at":"t.milliner_account_made"}},"raised":84,"quote":"made out by Madame Lesurier, of Bond Street, to William Derbyshire","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":84,"via":"always"},{"value":"a friend of Straker's","available":84,"via":"direct","quote":"Derbyshire was a friend of her husband’s","out":225,"by":"f.derbyshire_mythical"},{"value":"straker","available":84,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":84,"text":"Letters for this 'friend' come to Straker's own house, and his bill was in Straker's own pocket; the name may be one Straker used himself."}}],"answer":"straker","answer_given_at":229,"answer_given_quote":"who had recognised Straker as an excellent customer of the name of Derbyshire","answer_inferable_at":161,"answer_reasoning":{"para":161,"text":"Derbyshire's letters came to Straker's house and his bill was in Straker's pocket; Mrs. Straker never had the costly dress; and Holmes asks for Straker's photograph before going to London, where the Bond Street milliner is."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.madam_derbyshire","label":"Who is the woman whose costly dresses are on the milliner's account?","entry":{"of":"madam_derbyshire","attr":"identity","time":{"at":"t.milliner_account_made"}},"raised":85,"quote":"Madam Derbyshire had somewhat expensive tastes","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":85,"via":"always"},{"value":"the wife of William Derbyshire","available":85,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":85,"text":"The account is made out to Derbyshire, so the dresses would be for his wife."}},{"value":"mrs_straker","available":85,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":85,"text":"The bill was in Straker's pocket, so the clothes may have been for his own wife."},"out":225,"by":"f.dress_not_hers"},{"value":"a woman Straker kept under the name Derbyshire","available":92,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":92,"text":"Mrs. Straker says she never had such a dress, yet the bill for one was in her husband's pocket under another man's name."}}],"answer":"a woman Straker kept under the name Derbyshire","also_right":["the wife of William Derbyshire"],"answer_given_at":225,"answer_given_quote":"Straker was leading a double life, and keeping a second establishment","answer_inferable_at":92,"answer_reasoning":{"para":92,"text":"Mrs. Straker says she never had such a dress, yet the bill for it was in her husband's pocket under another man's name, and that man's letters came to his house."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.plymouth_meeting","label":"Did Holmes really meet Mrs. Straker at a garden-party in Plymouth?","entry":{"of":"mrs_straker","attr":"location","time":{"at":"t.plymouth_party"}},"raised":89,"quote":"Surely I met you in Plymouth at a garden-party some little time ago, Mrs. Straker?","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":89,"via":"always"},{"value":"yes, and she denies it","available":89,"via":"direct","quote":"Surely I met you in Plymouth at a garden-party","out":225,"by":"f.holmes_questions_mrs_straker"},{"value":"no: Holmes mistook her for someone else","available":90,"via":"direct","quote":"No, sir; you are mistaken.","out":225,"by":"f.holmes_questions_mrs_straker"},{"value":"no: Holmes said it on purpose, to learn whether she owns such a dress","available":91,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":91,"text":"He has just come from London and has just studied a bill for a costly costume; he then describes an expensive dress and draws her denial."}}],"answer":"no: Holmes said it on purpose, to learn whether she owns such a dress","answer_given_at":225,"answer_given_quote":"I questioned Mrs. Straker as to the dress without her knowing it","answer_inferable_at":93,"answer_reasoning":{"para":93,"text":"On her denial Holmes says 'that quite settles it' and leaves at once, as if the answer about the dress was what he wanted, not a social memory."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.candle_why","label":"Why did Straker carry a candle and matches when he went out that night?","entry":{"of":"straker","attr":"intends","time":{"at":"t.straker_leaves"}},"raised":78,"quote":"two inches of tallow candle","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":78,"via":"always"},{"value":"ordinary pocket things of a pipe-smoker","available":78,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":78,"text":"He also carried a pipe and tobacco; the matches served for that, and the candle-end may be habit."},"out":226,"by":"f.straker_strikes_light"},{"value":"he expected to need a small light out of doors","available":78,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":78,"text":"He was going to a stable where the lad on duty had a light; a candle suggests he meant to need light somewhere else, out on the moor."}}],"answer":"he expected to need a small light out of doors","answer_given_at":224,"answer_given_quote":"that was why he needed the candle, and struck the match","answer_inferable_at":104,"answer_reasoning":{"para":104,"text":"Holmes was looking for a burnt match in the hollow and found one, so a light was struck out on the moor, where Straker died with a candle and vestas in his pockets."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.who_struck_vesta","label":"Who struck the match found buried in the mud of the hollow, and why?","entry":{"of":"?","attr":"action","time":{"at":"t.vesta_dropped"}},"raised":102,"quote":"It was a wax vesta half burned","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":102,"via":"always"},{"value":"Straker, to make a light for something he meant to do in the hollow","available":102,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":102,"text":"Straker's pockets held a box of vestas and a candle-end, and he died in this hollow on a dark, wet night; a match struck here most likely came from his box."}},{"value":"Straker, to light his pipe","available":102,"via":"indirect","from":"f.straker_had_pipe","out":226,"by":"f.straker_strikes_light"},{"value":"the man who attacked Straker","available":102,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":102,"text":"Someone else was in the hollow that night, and he too may have struck a light there."},"out":226,"by":"f.straker_strikes_light"},{"value":"an old match dropped by someone else, with no part in the night's events","available":102,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":102,"text":"Many feet have trampled the hollow since Monday, so a match in the mud need not belong to the night of the crime."},"out":226,"by":"f.straker_strikes_light"}],"answer":"Straker, to make a light for something he meant to do in the hollow","answer_given_at":226,"answer_given_quote":"he had got behind the horse and had struck a light","answer_inferable_at":104,"answer_reasoning":{"para":104,"text":"Holmes expected the match, having seen Straker's vestas and candle; on a wet, dark night a match struck in the hollow most likely came from Straker, who needed light there."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.hollow_tracks","label":"Whose tracks are in the mud of the hollow?","entry":{"of":"?","attr":"location","time":{"at":"t.crime"}},"raised":107,"quote":"compared the impressions of each of them with marks upon the ground","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":107,"via":"always"},{"value":"Straker's and the horse's only","available":107,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":107,"text":"Gregory brought Straker's boot and the horse's shoe; if only those two match, no one else was in the hollow with them."}},{"value":"simpson","available":107,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":107,"text":"Gregory brought Simpson's shoe to compare; if it matches, Simpson was in the hollow, as the police believe."}},{"value":"a third person's, matching none of the footwear brought","available":107,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":107,"text":"Straker fought someone in the hollow; if the attacker was not Simpson, his prints would match neither the boot nor the shoe."}}],"answer":"Straker's and the horse's only","answer_given_at":226,"answer_given_quote":"Straker had led out the horse to a hollow where his light would be invisible.","answer_inferable_at":null,"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.horse_tracks","label":"How did Silver Blaze leave the hollow without leaving tracks?","entry":{"of":"silver_blaze","attr":"location","time":{"after":"t.crime","before":"t.tue_hollow"}},"raised":108,"quote":"I am afraid that there are no more tracks","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":108,"via":"always"},{"value":"the tracks are there, but further off than a hundred yards or missed by the search","available":108,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":108,"text":"Gregory looked only a hundred yards each way; Holmes crawls about the rim and means to walk the moor himself, as if he does not trust the negative result."}},{"value":"he went over ground that takes no prints","available":108,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":108,"text":"A shod horse leaves prints in soft mud but not on hard or stony ground; the moor beyond the hollow may not hold them."}}],"answer":"he went over ground that takes no prints","also_right":["the tracks are there, but further off than a hundred yards or missed by the search"],"answer_given_at":116,"answer_given_quote":"This part of the moor, as the Inspector remarked, is very hard and dry.","answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.will_run","label":"Will Silver Blaze run in the Wessex Cup on Tuesday?","entry":{"of":"silver_blaze","attr":"action","time":{"at":"t.race"}},"raised":110,"quote":"remove our horse’s name from the entries for the Cup","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":110,"via":"always"},{"value":"no: he is withdrawn, or still missing","available":110,"via":"direct","quote":"whether we do not owe it to the public to remove our horse’s name from the entries for the Cup","out":202,"by":"f.bay_is_sb"},{"value":"yes: he is found and fit by Tuesday","available":111,"via":"direct","quote":"I should let the name stand."}],"answer":"yes: he is found and fit by Tuesday","answer_given_at":202,"answer_given_quote":"he is the same old Silver Blaze as ever","answer_inferable_at":140,"answer_reasoning":{"para":140,"text":"The horse's tracks went in at Mapleton, Brown is cowed and promises 'it shall be there', and Holmes had told Ross to let the name stand."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.tracked_man","label":"Who is the man whose track joins the horse's near Mapleton?","entry":{"of":"tracked_man","attr":"identity","time":{"at":"t.man_with_horse"}},"raised":119,"quote":"A man’s track was visible beside the horse’s.","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":119,"via":"always"},{"value":"someone from the Mapleton stables","available":119,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":119,"text":"The man's track first appears quite close to Mapleton, where the horse came alone, so he was most likely someone who belonged there."}},{"value":"silas_brown","available":127,"via":"indirect","from":"f.dawson_says_first_stirring"},{"value":"dawson","available":124,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":124,"text":"A Mapleton groom runs out from the gates where the track ends; a groom might have caught a stray horse."},"out":146,"by":"f.tracked_man_brown"},{"value":"the man who was with Straker in the hollow, following the horse","available":119,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":119,"text":"Someone fought Straker in the hollow. {"id":"q.why_turned_back","label":"Why did the man lead the horse towards King's Pyland and then back to Mapleton?","entry":{"of":"tracked_man","attr":"intends","time":{"at":"t.man_horse_back"}},"raised":122,"quote":"the same tracks coming back again in the opposite direction","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":122,"via":"always"},{"value":"he set out to return the stray, then decided to keep it out of the race","available":122,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":122,"text":"Heading for King's Pyland looks like returning a stray to its stable. Turning back suggests he changed his mind once he saw what keeping the favourite would gain him."}},{"value":"to lay a false trail","available":122,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":122,"text":"A track that goes out and comes back on itself could be meant to mislead anyone following it."},"out":146,"by":"f.brown_intends_hide"}],"answer":"he set out to return the stray, then decided to keep it out of the race","answer_given_at":146,"answer_given_quote":"his first impulse had been to lead him back to King’s Pyland","answer_inferable_at":124,"answer_reasoning":{"para":124,"text":"The double track heads for King's Pyland and then comes back to the gates of the rival stable, whose horse gains if the favourite is missing."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.five_am","label":"Why does Holmes ask whether Silas Brown is about at five in the morning?","entry":{"of":"holmes","attr":"intends","time":{"at":"t.thu_mapleton_gates"}},"raised":126,"quote":"if I were to call at five o’clock to-morrow morning?","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":126,"via":"always"},{"value":"to learn who would have met a stray horse at Mapleton at dawn","available":126,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":126,"text":"A horse that strayed from the hollow in the night would reach Mapleton early in the morning, and the first man up would be the one to find it."}},{"value":"he really means to call at that hour","available":126,"via":"direct","quote":"Should I be too early to see your master, Mr. Silas Brown"}],"answer":"to learn who would have met a stray horse at Mapleton at dawn","answer_given_at":146,"answer_given_quote":"when according to his custom he was the first down","answer_inferable_at":127,"answer_reasoning":{"para":127,"text":"The tracks end at Mapleton's gates; the answer that Brown is always the first stirring tells Holmes who would have met a horse arriving at dawn."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.whisper","label":"What did Holmes whisper in Silas Brown's ear?","entry":{"of":"holmes","attr":"says","time":{"at":"t.thu_mapleton_gates"}},"raised":132,"quote":"Holmes leaned forward and whispered something in the trainer’s ear.","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":132,"via":"always"},{"value":"that Brown took Silver Blaze into Mapleton","available":132,"via":"indirect","from":"f.tracks_end_asphalt"},{"value":"that Brown killed Straker","available":132,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":132,"text":"Brown had bets on the race and was no friend to Straker; a charge of murder would explain his violent start."},"out":146,"by":"f.brown_sees_horse"}],"answer":"that Brown took Silver Blaze into Mapleton","answer_given_at":146,"answer_given_quote":"I described to him so exactly what his actions had been upon that morning","answer_inferable_at":137,"answer_reasoning":{"para":137,"text":"Holmes has just followed the tracks to Mapleton's gates; after the whisper Brown is broken and promises 'it shall all be done', as a man caught holding the horse would."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.brown_it","label":"What is the 'it' that Silas Brown promises 'shall be there', and where?","entry":{"of":"silas_brown","attr":"intends","time":{"at":"t.brown_carries_out"}},"raised":140,"quote":"It shall be there.","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":140,"via":"always"},{"value":"Silver Blaze, at the racecourse for the Wessex Cup","available":140,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":140,"text":"The tracks led the horse into Mapleton, and Holmes has told Ross to keep him entered for Tuesday; 'there' may be the race."}},{"value":"Silver Blaze, returned to King's Pyland","available":140,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":140,"text":"If Brown has the horse, the plain remedy is to send him back to his own stable."},"out":202,"by":"f.bay_is_sb"}],"answer":"Silver Blaze, at the racecourse for the Wessex Cup","answer_given_at":202,"answer_given_quote":"he is the same old Silver Blaze as ever","answer_inferable_at":161,"answer_reasoning":{"para":161,"text":"Holmes tells Ross he hopes the horse will start on Tuesday and to have his jockey ready, so 'there' is the race."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.change_it","label":"What does Brown mean by asking whether he should 'change it' first?","entry":{"of":"silas_brown","attr":"action","time":{"at":"t.brown_carries_out"}},"raised":140,"quote":"Should I change it first or not?","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":140,"via":"always"},{"value":"undo some change to the horse's looks so that he is known again","available":140,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":140,"text":"The police searched Mapleton and found nothing, yet the horse went in there. He may have been altered so as not to be recognised, and 'change it' would mean putting him back as he was."}},{"value":"alter the horse in some way before handing him over","available":140,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":140,"text":"Brown may be offering to disguise the horse so that it can be moved without being recognised."},"out":202,"by":"f.sb_disguise"}],"answer":"undo some change to the horse's looks so that he is known again","answer_given_at":202,"answer_given_quote":"You have only to wash his face and his leg in spirits of wine","answer_inferable_at":148,"answer_reasoning":{"para":148,"text":"Brown knew the horse by his white forehead, the police searched Mapleton and missed him, and Holmes calls Brown an old horse-faker: the horse has been altered, and 'change it' means putting him back."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.holmes_secret","label":"Why does Holmes settle matters privately with Brown instead of recovering the horse at once?","entry":{"of":"holmes","attr":"intends","time":{"at":"t.thu_brown_reappears"}},"raised":141,"quote":"I shall write to you about it.","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":141,"via":"always"},{"value":"to produce the horse in his own way and at his own time","available":141,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":141,"text":"He laughs at Brown's question and says he will write. He seems to have a plan for how and when the horse appears, perhaps with the impatient Colonel in mind."}},{"value":"he wants the murder solved first","available":141,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":114,"text":"Holmes set the murder aside to find the horse; with the horse placed, he may hold it back until the rest is clear."}},{"value":"to spare Brown in return for his help","available":141,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":141,"text":"Brown is cowed and obedient; Holmes may be trading silence for the horse's safe return."}}],"answer":"to produce the horse in his own way and at his own time","also_right":["to spare Brown in return for his help"],"answer_given_at":152,"answer_given_quote":"I am inclined now to have a little amusement at his expense.","answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.how_hidden","label":"How did Silver Blaze escape the police search of the Mapleton stables?","entry":{"of":"silver_blaze","attr":"condition","time":{"at":"t.mapleton_examined"}},"raised":147,"quote":"But his stables had been searched?","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":147,"via":"always"},{"value":"disguised by a horse-faker's trick, so the searchers did not know him","available":148,"via":"direct","quote":"an old horse-faker like him has many a dodge"},{"value":"hidden somewhere the searchers did not look","available":147,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":147,"text":"A stable yard has many corners and out-buildings; a horse could be kept out of the way while the police were there."},"out":202,"by":"f.sb_disguise"},{"value":"his white forehead was covered or dyed, and he stood among the other horses","available":147,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":146,"text":"Brown knew the horse by the white forehead that gives him his name, and he asked Holmes whether to 'change it' first; covering the blaze would leave an ordinary-looking horse."}}],"answer":"disguised by a horse-faker's trick, so the searchers did not know him","also_right":["his white forehead was covered or dyed, and he stood among the other horses"],"answer_given_at":202,"answer_given_quote":"You have only to wash his face and his leg in spirits of wine","answer_inferable_at":148,"answer_reasoning":{"para":148,"text":"Holmes calls Brown an old horse-faker with many a dodge, and Brown asked whether to 'change it' first; the horse known by his white forehead was altered so the searchers did not know him."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.why_london","label":"Why does Holmes go back to London at once instead of pursuing Straker's murderer on the spot?","entry":{"of":"holmes","attr":"intends","time":{"at":"t.thu_walk_kp"}},"raised":156,"quote":"we both go back to London by the night train","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":156,"via":"always"},{"value":"he has given up on the murder","available":160,"via":"direct","quote":"So you despair of arresting the murderer of poor Straker","out":229,"by":"f.holmes_calls_milliner"},{"value":"to make inquiries in London, at the Bond Street milliner or at Weiss & Co.","available":156,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":156,"text":"The two leads that point to London are the milliner's bill made out to William Derbyshire by Madame Lesurier of Bond Street, and the cataract knife marked Weiss & Co., London."}},{"value":"he already believes he knows what happened and needs nothing more on the moor","available":156,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":154,"text":"He calls the horse a minor point beside the murder, yet he leaves without any further search, as if the rest needs only to be confirmed."}}],"answer":"to make inquiries in London, at the Bond Street milliner or at Weiss & Co.","also_right":["he already believes he knows what happened and needs nothing more on the moor"],"answer_given_at":229,"answer_given_quote":"When I returned to London I called upon the milliner","answer_inferable_at":161,"answer_reasoning":{"para":161,"text":"Holmes asks for Straker's photograph just before leaving for London, where the milliner who made out Derbyshire's bill keeps her shop."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.holmes_murder_knows","label":"Does Holmes know who killed John Straker?","entry":{"of":"holmes","attr":"knows","time":{"at":"t.thu_back_villa"}},"raised":160,"quote":"So you despair of arresting the murderer of poor Straker","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":160,"via":"always"},{"value":"no: there are grave difficulties","available":161,"via":"direct","quote":"There are certainly grave difficulties in the way","out":206,"by":"f.holmes_says_done_so"},{"value":"yes, or nearly, but he keeps it to himself","available":160,"via":"indirect","from":"f.holmes_says_own_methods"}],"answer":"yes, or nearly, but he keeps it to himself","answer_given_at":206,"answer_given_quote":"I have done so","answer_inferable_at":160,"answer_from":"f.holmes_says_own_methods","answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.photo_why","label":"What does Holmes want the photograph of John Straker for?","entry":{"of":"holmes","attr":"intends","time":{"at":"t.thu_back_villa"}},"raised":161,"quote":"Might I ask for a photograph of Mr. John Straker?","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":161,"via":"always"},{"value":"to ask at the Bond Street milliner whether Straker is 'William Derbyshire'","available":161,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":161,"text":"He is going to London, where the milliner who made out Derbyshire's bill keeps her shop, and Derbyshire's letters came to Straker's house."}},{"value":"to show to some other witness who may have seen Straker","available":161,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":161,"text":"A photograph is for recognition; someone, perhaps in London or on the moor, may be able to say where Straker had been."}}],"answer":"to ask at the Bond Street milliner whether Straker is 'William Derbyshire'","answer_given_at":225,"answer_given_quote":"by calling there with Straker’s photograph I could easily dispose of the mythical Derbyshire","answer_inferable_at":161,"answer_reasoning":{"para":161,"text":"He is going to London, where the milliner who billed 'Derbyshire' has her shop, and Derbyshire's letters came to Straker's house while his wife never had the dress."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.maid_question","label":"What does Holmes ask the maid before he leaves?","entry":{"of":"holmes","attr":"says","time":{"at":"t.thu_holmes_maid"}},"raised":163,"quote":"I have a question which I should like to put to the maid.","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":163,"via":"always"},{"value":"something about Hunter's curried mutton, how it was chosen or prepared","available":163,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":163,"text":"The maid carried the drugged supper, and only Hunter's portion held opium."}},{"value":"something about Straker's habits, his knife or the dresses","available":163,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":163,"text":"As the household's servant she would know Straker's ways and the clothes in the house better than anyone."}},{"value":"something about the stranger on the path that night","available":163,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":163,"text":"She is the one witness who spoke with the stranger and saw his packet."}}],"answer":null,"answer_given_at":null,"answer_given_quote":null,"answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.sheep_lame","label":"Why have three of the paddock sheep gone lame?","entry":{"of":"sheep","attr":"condition","time":{"from":"t.sheep_lame"}},"raised":173,"quote":"three of them have gone lame, sir","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":173,"via":"always"},{"value":"an ordinary ailment, of no account","available":173,"via":"direct","quote":"not of much account","out":228,"by":"f.straker_practises"},{"value":"someone practised a delicate cut on their legs before trying it on the horse","available":175,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":175,"text":"Holmes is delighted and calls it a singular epidemic. Taken with the cataract knife, the candle and the horse led out to a hollow, lame sheep look like practice for laming the horse."}}],"answer":"someone practised a delicate cut on their legs before trying it on the horse","answer_given_at":228,"answer_given_quote":"so astute a man as Straker would not undertake this delicate tendon-nicking without a little practice","answer_inferable_at":175,"answer_reasoning":{"para":175,"text":"Holmes expected the answer and is delighted; with the cataract knife, the candle and the horse led out to a hollow, lame sheep look like rehearsal for laming the horse."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.dog_silent_why","label":"Why did the stable dog make no noise in the night?","entry":{"of":"dog","attr":"condition","time":{"after":"t.mon_hunter_runs_out","before":"t.tue_at_stables"}},"raised":182,"quote":"That was the curious incident","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":182,"via":"always"},{"value":"the man who came for the horse was someone the dog knew","available":182,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":182,"text":"A guard dog barks at strangers; it had been loosed against Simpson that very evening, yet it was silent when the horse was taken."}},{"value":"the dog was drugged or shut away","available":182,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":182,"text":"The watchman was drugged; the dog might have been put out of action too."},"out":219,"by":"f.dog_knew_visitor"}],"answer":"the man who came for the horse was someone the dog knew","answer_given_at":219,"answer_given_quote":"Obviously the midnight visitor was some one whom the dog knew well.","answer_inferable_at":182,"answer_reasoning":{"para":182,"text":"The dog was loosed on the stranger that very evening, yet it made no noise when the horse was taken out; it knew the visitor."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.who_backed_horse","label":"Who has been backing Silver Blaze so heavily that his price has shortened?","entry":{"of":"?","attr":"action","time":{"after":"t.race_eve_betting","before":"t.race_drive"}},"raised":188,"quote":"that is the curious part of it","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":188,"via":"always"},{"value":"silas_brown","available":188,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":188,"text":"Brown has the horse and has promised Holmes it 'shall be there'; he knows it will run and could hedge his bets on Desborough by backing it."}},{"value":"holmes","available":188,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":188,"text":"Holmes alone knows the horse is found and will start; a man who knows that can back him at fifteen to one."}},{"value":"someone who has heard that the horse is found","available":189,"via":"direct","quote":"Somebody knows something, that is clear."}],"answer":null,"answer_given_at":null,"answer_given_quote":null,"answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.bay_identity","label":"Which horse is the bay running in Colonel Ross's colours?","entry":{"of":"the_bay_horse","attr":"identity","time":{"at":"t.race_canter"}},"raised":198,"quote":"That’s not my horse","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":198,"via":"always"},{"value":"a different horse running in Silver Blaze's place","available":198,"via":"direct","quote":"That’s not my horse","out":202,"by":"f.bay_is_sb"},{"value":"silver_blaze","available":198,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":198,"text":"Ross scratched his other horse, so only Silver Blaze runs in his colours. Holmes asked whether Ross would know his horse, and Brown had asked whether to 'change it' first: the blaze may be dyed over."}}],"answer":"silver_blaze","answer_given_at":202,"answer_given_quote":"he is the same old Silver Blaze as ever","answer_inferable_at":198,"answer_reasoning":{"para":198,"text":"Ross scratched his other horse, so the bay in his colours must be Silver Blaze; Holmes asked whether Ross would know him, and Brown had asked whether to 'change it': the blaze is dyed."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.straker_unworthy","label":"In what way was John Straker unworthy of the Colonel's confidence?","entry":{"of":"straker","attr":"action","time":{"after":"t.straker_trainer","before":"t.crime"}},"raised":214,"quote":"John Straker was a man who was entirely unworthy of your confidence","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":214,"via":"always"},{"value":"he meant to harm the horse himself so that it would lose the race","available":214,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":214,"text":"The horse struck in self-defence, so Straker was attacking it; he had taken a cataract knife, a candle and matches to the hollow."}},{"value":"he led a secret life as William Derbyshire, beyond his means","available":214,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":92,"text":"A costly dress bill for 'Derbyshire' was in his pocket, and his wife never had such a dress."}},{"value":"he drugged the stable-lad himself","available":214,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":182,"text":"The dog knew the night visitor; the man who needed the watchman asleep may have been Straker."}}],"answer":"he meant to harm the horse himself so that it would lose the race","also_right":["he led a secret life as William Derbyshire, beyond his means","he drugged the stable-lad himself"],"answer_given_at":221,"answer_given_quote":"And it was to be used for a delicate operation that night.","answer_inferable_at":214,"answer_reasoning":{"para":214,"text":"The horse struck in self-defence, so Straker was attacking it, with a cataract knife, candle and match in the hollow, after lame sheep in the paddock."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.self_defence","label":"What was Straker doing to the horse when it struck him?","entry":{"of":"straker","attr":"action","time":{"at":"t.crime"}},"raised":214,"quote":"it was done in self-defence","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":214,"via":"always"},{"value":"cutting at its leg with the cataract knife by the light of a match","available":214,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":214,"text":"He had his coat off, a struck vesta and an uncorked fine blade in the hollow, and lame sheep in the paddock suggest practice at a cut to the leg."}},{"value":"beating or mishandling it","available":214,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":214,"text":"Any rough handling in the dark could make a frightened horse lash out."},"out":226,"by":"f.straker_strikes_light"},{"value":"about to nick its tendon, having struck a light behind it","available":226,"via":"direct","quote":"he had got behind the horse and had struck a light"}],"answer":"about to nick its tendon, having struck a light behind it","also_right":["cutting at its leg with the cataract knife by the light of a match"],"answer_given_at":226,"answer_given_quote":"he had got behind the horse and had struck a light","answer_inferable_at":214,"answer_reasoning":{"para":214,"text":"His coat was off, a vesta was struck in the hollow, the cataract knife was uncorked, and the sheep had gone lame: he was making a fine cut at the horse's leg."},"answer_strength":"likely"}
{"id":"q.how_unseen","label":"How did the opium get into Hunter's dish without the maid seeing?","entry":{"of":"?","attr":"action","time":{"at":"t.opium_added"}},"raised":218,"quote":"Which of them, then, had access to that dish without the maid seeing them?","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":218,"via":"always"},{"value":"Straker slipped it into the boy's portion in the kitchen once it was set aside","available":218,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":218,"text":"The opium went in after Hunter's dish was set apart. Straker chose the curry and was in the house, and he was the one who would profit from the lad sleeping."}},{"value":"Mrs. Straker added it, knowing or not knowing what it was","available":218,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":218,"text":"Holmes names her with her husband as one of the only two who could have chosen curried mutton that night."}}],"answer":null,"answer_given_at":null,"answer_given_quote":null,"answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.mrs_straker_knew","label":"Did Mrs. Straker know of her husband's plot against the horse?","entry":{"of":"mrs_straker","attr":"knows","time":{"at":"t.crime"}},"raised":218,"quote":"our attention centres upon Straker and his wife","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":218,"via":"always"},{"value":"no: she knew nothing of it","available":218,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":92,"text":"She never had the costly dress on Derbyshire's bill and begged her husband not to go out; she seems to have been kept in the dark."}},{"value":"yes: she helped by drugging the dish","available":218,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":218,"text":"She is one of the two who could have chosen the curry, and Holmes asks which of the two had access to the dish."}}],"answer":null,"answer_given_at":null,"answer_given_quote":null,"answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.cravat_purpose","label":"Why did Straker pick up Simpson's dropped cravat?","entry":{"of":"straker","attr":"intends","time":{"at":"t.straker_picks_cravat"}},"raised":226,"quote":"Straker had picked it up","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":226,"via":"always"},{"value":"to use it in securing the horse's leg","available":226,"via":"direct","quote":"with some idea, perhaps, that he might use it in securing the horse’s leg"},{"value":"to leave it where it would throw suspicion on the tout","available":226,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":226,"text":"A man planning a crime that night might keep a stranger's cravat to point the blame at him if anything went wrong."}}],"answer":null,"answer_given_at":null,"answer_given_quote":null,"answer_inferable_at":null}
{"id":"q.straker_bets","label":"Had Straker laid money against his own horse?","entry":{"of":"straker","attr":"action","time":{"after":"t.straker_double_life","before":"t.crime"}},"raised":220,"quote":"trainers have made sure of great sums of money by laying against their own horses, through agents","possibilities":[{"value":"something else","available":220,"via":"always"},{"value":"yes, through agents","available":220,"via":"direct","quote":"by laying against their own horses, through agents"},{"value":"no: he was paid by others who wanted the favourite to lose","available":220,"via":"indirect","reasoning":{"para":220,"text":"No bets of Straker's are shown; a man deep in debt could equally have been paid by others with money against the horse."}}],"answer":null,"answer_given_at":null,"answer_given_quote":null,"answer_inferable_at":null}

## The annotated reading

{"bk":"bk.favourite_motive","text":"When a horse is favourite for a big race, people who have bet against it, or who stand to lose if it wins, have a reason to stop it running.","exceptions":"The owner or backers of the favourite normally want it to run.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.horse_conspicuous","text":"A famous racehorse is large, valuable and recognisable, and it is hard to hide or move unseen in open, thinly settled country.","exceptions":"It can be hidden if it is disguised, kept in a building, or killed.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.genre_detective_signals","text":"In detective stories, what the detective asks for, looks at, or finds odd usually matters later.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.period_gypsies","text":"Readers of the period readily suspected roaming gypsies of horse-stealing.","exceptions":"It is a prejudice of the time, not evidence, and a large reward puts the same people on the side of finding the horse.","reader":"period"}
{"bk":"bk.drug_taste","text":"A drug mixed into food is easier to give unnoticed in a strongly flavoured dish than in a plain one, because most drugs have a taste or smell of their own.","exceptions":"Tasteless drugs; a very small dose; a careless or very hungry eater.","reader":"specialist"}
{"bk":"bk.keyholder","text":"The master of a stable or house normally keeps a key to it.","exceptions":"Keys may be held only by the servant on duty, or kept at a separate office.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.guard_dog","text":"A guard dog kept in a building gives the alarm when strangers come at night.","exceptions":"It is drugged, removed, or out of earshot, or it knows the visitor.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.self_wound","text":"A man struck hard on the head can thrash about convulsively and may hurt himself with anything he is holding.","reader":"specialist"}
{"bk":"bk.lost_item","text":"Something lost out of doors can be picked up and carried off by anyone who comes across it, so where it turns up later does not prove where its owner went.","exceptions":"It was lost only moments before, or in a place nobody else could reach.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.search_by_description","text":"Searchers look for what they have been told to look for, so a search finds a thing only if it still looks the way it was described.","exceptions":"Searchers who know the object closely, or who check it in ways that do not depend on appearance.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.tout_bribe","text":"Racing touts commonly paid stable-lads money for inside information on how the horses were running.","reader":"period"}
{"bk":"bk.cataract_knife","text":"A cataract knife is an eye-surgeon's instrument: a very thin, rigid, extremely sharp blade made for tiny, precise cuts, not for fighting.","reader":"specialist"}
{"bk":"bk.alias_mail","text":"When a man has letters for a 'friend' sent to his own house, the friend may be the man himself under another name.","exceptions":"A genuine friend who has no fixed address, or who has a real reason to receive post elsewhere.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.second_household","text":"A man secretly paying for costly women's clothes, under a name not his own, is often keeping a woman his wife does not know about, and such a life needs more money than his wages.","exceptions":"Gifts to a relative; a debt he is settling for someone else.","reader":"period"}
{"bk":"bk.light_outdoors","text":"Someone who carries a candle and matches at night means to need a small light somewhere, usually out of the wind and away from other lamps.","exceptions":"He carries them by habit, or to light a pipe.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.detective_feint","text":"A detective may make a false remark on purpose so that the answer reveals something the person would not volunteer.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.no_wind","text":"On a still night a garment does not end up on a bush by itself. If it is found draped there, someone put it there.","exceptions":"It was caught and dragged during a struggle, or thrown and snagged by chance.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.coat_off","text":"A man takes off a heavy coat in bad weather when he needs his arms free for close work or for exertion.","exceptions":"It is torn off him, or he is too hot from running.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.hoofprints","text":"A shod horse moving over soft, wet ground leaves hoof-prints that a careful searcher can follow, and a cast shoe can be matched against them.","exceptions":"Hard or stony ground, heavy trampling, or ground searched too narrowly.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.scratch_race","text":"An owner withdraws a horse from a race when he believes it cannot run. Someone who advises keeping the entry expects the horse to be back and fit in time.","exceptions":"Keeping the entry as a bluff, or to avoid losing a forfeit.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.horse_herd","text":"A horse is a herd animal. Left loose, it tends to make for its own stable or for other horses nearby rather than roam alone.","exceptions":"It is badly frightened, injured, shut in, or caught by someone.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.horse_markings_dyed","text":"A horse's distinctive markings, such as a white blaze, can be dyed or painted over so that it passes for another horse, and later washed back.","exceptions":"Searchers who know the horse closely by build, gait or temperament.","reader":"period"}
{"bk":"bk.photo_identify","text":"A detective who asks for someone's photograph usually means to show it to a witness who may recognise that person, perhaps under another name.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.practise_first","text":"Someone who must perform a delicate operation he has never done before tends to practise it first on something less valuable.","exceptions":"A practised hand; no time or opportunity to rehearse.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.subtle_laming","text":"A small cut to a horse's leg tendon can make it go slightly lame, so that it runs badly without any obvious wound.","reader":"specialist"}
{"bk":"bk.horse_kick","text":"A horse that is hurt or frightened at close quarters can kick with enough force to kill a man.","reader":"all"}
{"bk":"bk.odds_shorten","text":"When a horse's price shortens sharply just before a race, with nothing announced, it usually means that people with inside knowledge are backing it heavily.","exceptions":"A rumour that turns out false; general public enthusiasm; a bookmaker cutting his exposure.","reader":"period"}
{"id":"t4.1","para":4,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"the singular disappearance of the favourite for the Wessex Cup, and the tragic murder of its trainer","text":"Two events: the favourite for the Wessex Cup has vanished, and its trainer has been murdered."}
{"id":"t4.2","para":4,"tag":"QUESTION","confidence":"fair","profile":"typical","from":["t4.1"],"text":"How are the disappearance and the murder connected?","options":["A thief came for the horse and killed the trainer who got in the way","The two are separate, or only linked by coincidence","The trainer was mixed up in what happened to the horse"]}
{"id":"t4.3","para":4,"tag":"HYPOTHESIS","confidence":"weak","profile":"typical","from":["t4.1"],"text":"What does 'disappearance' mean for the horse?","options":["Stolen and hidden","Strayed loose in the confusion","Killed or maimed and the body not yet found"]}
{"id":"t4.4","para":4,"tag":"QUESTION","subtype":"motive","confidence":"fair","profile":"typical","from":["t4.1","bk.favourite_motive"],"text":"Who gains if the favourite does not run? Bookmakers, people who bet against it, rival owners. Who would want the trainer dead?","options":["Someone with money riding against the favourite","A rival stable","Someone with a private grudge against the trainer"]}
{"id":"t6.1","para":6,"tag":"GENRE","subtype":"watch-detective","confidence":"weak","profile":"careful","from":["bk.genre_detective_signals"],"reads":"You would oblige me by bringing with you your very excellent field-glass.","text":"Holmes wants a field-glass before he has seen the ground. He expects to look at something at a distance: open moor, a racecourse, figures far off. Keep it in mind."}
{"id":"t6.2","para":6,"tag":"GENRE","confidence":"weak","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"there are points about the case which promise to make it an absolutely unique one","text":"Holmes already sees particular 'points', apparently from the newspaper reports alone. The clues may be in the facts he is about to list."}
{"id":"t8.1","para":8,"tag":"SET ASIDE","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"Our rate at present is fifty-three and a half miles an hour.","text":"The speed calculation from the telegraph posts shows off Holmes's method. It has no bearing on the case."}
{"id":"t10.1","para":10,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":["t4.1"],"reads":"this matter of the murder of John Straker and the disappearance of Silver Blaze","text":"Names: the trainer is John Straker, and the horse is Silver Blaze."}
{"id":"t12.1","para":12,"tag":"GENRE","confidence":"fair","profile":"careful","from":[],"reads":"the art of the reasoner should be used rather for the sifting of details than for the acquiring of fresh evidence","text":"Holmes says the answer lies in the facts already known, once they are cleared of journalists' theorising. So the reader should treat his coming summary as the evidence to sift, and distrust press 'conjecture'."}
{"id":"t12.2","para":12,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"I received telegrams from both Colonel Ross, the owner of the horse, and from Inspector Gregory, who is looking after the case, inviting my co-operation","text":"Colonel Ross owns the horse, and Inspector Gregory leads the police case. Both called Holmes in on Tuesday evening."}
{"id":"t13.1","para":13,"tag":"INFER","confidence":"strong","profile":"careful","from":["t12.2"],"reads":"And this is Thursday morning.","text":"The telegrams came Tuesday evening, so the crime happened no later than Tuesday, probably Monday night or Tuesday. Two days have now passed."}
{"id":"t14.1","para":14,"tag":"DEFAULT","confidence":"fair","profile":"typical","from":["t4.3","bk.horse_conspicuous"],"reads":"I could not believe it possible that the most remarkable horse in England could long remain concealed, especially in so sparsely inhabited a place as the north of Dartmoor","text":"Holmes reasoned as anyone would: a famous horse on an empty moor gets found quickly."}
{"id":"t14.2","para":14,"tag":"DROP","confidence":"fair","profile":"typical","from":["t4.3","t13.1","t14.1"],"relations":[{"type":"drops","target":"t4.3"}],"text":"The horse cannot have simply strayed. A loose favourite on a thinly peopled moor would have turned up within two days. Someone is keeping it out of sight, or it is dead."}
{"id":"t14.3","para":14,"tag":"QUESTION","confidence":"fair","profile":"careful","from":["t14.1","t14.2","bk.horse_conspicuous"],"text":"How can so conspicuous a horse stay unfound? Turn it around: what would make the usual rule fail here?","options":["Shut in a building nearby","Changed in appearance so that nobody recognises it","Taken a long way off very quickly","Dead, and the body hidden"]}
{"id":"t14.4","para":14,"tag":"HYPOTHESIS","subtype":"scenario","confidence":"fair","profile":"typical","from":["t4.2","t14.1"],"reads":"I expected to hear that he had been found, and that his abductor was the murderer of John Straker","text":"Working theory, Holmes's own until now: one person took the horse and killed Straker."}
{"id":"t14.5","para":14,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"beyond the arrest of young Fitzroy Simpson nothing had been done","text":"The police have arrested a young man, Fitzroy Simpson."}
{"id":"t14.6","para":14,"tag":"HYPOTHESIS","confidence":"weak","profile":"typical","from":["t14.5","t14.4"],"text":"Simpson is the abductor and murderer.","options":["Simpson did both","Simpson did one or the other","Simpson is innocent and was arrested on suspicion"]}
{"id":"t14.7","para":14,"tag":"WEIGH","confidence":"weak","profile":"careful","from":["t14.5","t14.6"],"relations":[{"type":"strains","target":"t14.6"}],"text":"Holmes treats the arrest as 'nothing having been done'. To him an arrest does not settle the case, especially with the horse still missing. If Simpson knew where the horse was, one might expect it to have been found by now."}
{"id":"t14.8","para":14,"tag":"GENRE","subtype":"watch-detective","confidence":"weak","profile":"careful","from":[],"reads":"Yet in some ways I feel that yesterday has not been wasted.","text":"The fact that the horse was not found is itself information to Holmes. Its continued absence is a clue to how it is being hidden."}
{"id":"t16.1","para":16,"tag":"RESIDUE","confidence":"fair","profile":"careful","from":["t4.2","t4.4","t14.3","t14.4","t14.6","t6.1"],"text":"Open at the end of the scene: how the murder and the disappearance connect; where an unfindable famous horse can be; who profits if the favourite does not run; whether Simpson is more than a convenient arrest; and what Holmes means to look at with the field-glass. The details have not been given yet. Holmes's list of facts comes next and should be read closely."}
{"id":"t16.2","para":16,"tag":"HYPOTHESIS","subtype":"scenario","confidence":"weak","profile":"typical","from":["t14.4","t14.2","t4.4"],"text":"Leading picture so far: someone with an interest against the favourite stole or harmed Silver Blaze, killed Straker in the process, and has hidden the horse well. Simpson is the police's candidate. It is held loosely, because Holmes's own version of it has already failed once."}
{"id":"t18.1","para":18,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":["t4.1"],"reads":"the betting being three to one on him","text":"Silver Blaze is heavily odds-on for the Wessex Cup."}
{"id":"t18.2","para":18,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"preventing Silver Blaze from being there at the fall of the flag next Tuesday","text":"The race is next Tuesday, about five days off. Whoever has the horse needs to keep it out of sight only until then."}
{"id":"t18.3","para":18,"tag":"INFER","subtype":"motive","confidence":"strong","profile":"typical","from":["t18.1","bk.favourite_motive"],"reads":"so that even at those odds enormous sums of money have been laid upon him","text":"Huge sums are bet on him, so the bookmakers who took those bets stand to lose heavily if he wins. Layers against the favourite are the obvious class of suspect."}
{"id":"t19.1","para":19,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":["t10.1"],"reads":"has always shown himself to be a zealous and honest servant","text":"Straker is a former jockey with twelve years' good service to the Colonel. His character is reported as excellent."}
{"id":"t19.2","para":19,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"One of these lads sat up each night in the stable, while the others slept in the loft","text":"Routine: one lad sits up in the stable each night and the other two sleep in the loft above."}
{"id":"t19.3","para":19,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"lived in a small villa about two hundred yards from the stables","text":"Map: Straker's house is 200 yards from the stables and a cluster of villas lies half a mile north. Tavistock is two miles west, and Mapleton is two miles across the moor. Everything else is empty moor."}
{"id":"t19.4","para":19,"tag":"HYPOTHESIS","subtype":"opportunity","confidence":"weak","profile":"careful","from":["t19.3","t14.3"],"reads":"the larger training establishment of Mapleton, which belongs to Lord Backwater, and is managed by Silas Brown","text":"A rival training stable two miles off is one place where a horse could stand unnoticed among other horses, under cover. It answers the 'shut in a building' option of t14.3.","options":["Mapleton is hiding the horse","Mapleton is mere geography"]}
{"id":"t19.5","para":19,"tag":"DEFAULT","subtype":"period","confidence":"weak","profile":"typical","from":["bk.period_gypsies","t14.3"],"reads":"inhabited only by a few roaming gypsies","text":"A reader of the time would think at once of the gypsies as horse-thieves. That is a candidate the story itself puts on the map."}
{"id":"t20.1","para":20,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"the stables were locked up at nine o’clock","text":"The stables were locked at nine. Ned Hunter stayed inside on guard, and the other two lads went up to the house for supper."}
{"id":"t20.2","para":20,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"which consisted of a dish of curried mutton","text":"Edith Baxter, the maid, carried Hunter's supper down to the stables. It was curried mutton."}
{"id":"t20.3","para":20,"tag":"NOTICE","confidence":"weak","profile":"careful","from":["t20.2"],"reads":"it was the rule that the lad on duty should drink nothing else","text":"The duty lad drinks only tap water. So if anyone wanted to dose him, the food is the only channel. The menu is told in unusual detail."}
{"id":"t21.1","para":21,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"carried a heavy stick with a knob to it","text":"The stranger is gentlemanly, wears a grey tweed suit and cloth cap with gaiters, and carries a heavy knobbed stick. He is pale and nervous, and a little over thirty."}
{"id":"t24.1","para":24,"tag":"WEIGH","confidence":"fair","profile":"typical","from":["t19.2"],"reads":"I understand that a stable-boy sleeps there alone every night.","text":"He claimed to be lost (¶22), yet he knows the stable routine. The 'lost' story is a pretext, and he came to this stable on purpose."}
{"id":"t24.2","para":24,"tag":"HYPOTHESIS","confidence":"fair","profile":"typical","from":["t24.1"],"reads":"He took a piece of white paper folded up out of his waistcoat pocket.","text":"What is in the folded paper?","options":["A drug for the boy's supper","A note or bribe-offer for the boy","Money"]}
{"id":"t25.1","para":25,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"It was already opened, and Hunter was seated at the small table inside.","text":"Meals are handed in through a window that was already open, with Hunter at a table just inside."}
{"id":"t26.1","para":26,"tag":"WEIGH","confidence":"weak","profile":"careful","from":["t24.2"],"reads":"The girl has sworn that as he spoke she noticed the corner of the little paper packet protruding from his closed hand.","text":"This is sworn, but it comes from a frightened girl by lantern light, and it fits what everyone later came to believe. It shows the packet was still in his hand. It does not show what was done with it."}
{"id":"t28.1","para":28,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"You’ve two horses in for the Wessex Cup—Silver Blaze and Bayard.","text":"King's Pyland has two runners. The stranger asks whether the stable secretly prefers Bayard. That is a tout's question."}
{"id":"t29.1","para":29,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"rushed across the stable to unloose the dog","text":"There is a guard dog kept in the stable."}
{"id":"t29.2","para":29,"tag":"INFER","subtype":"opportunity","confidence":"fair","profile":"typical","from":["t25.1","t26.1"],"reads":"as she ran she looked back and saw that the stranger was leaning through the window","text":"While Hunter crossed the stable for the dog, the stranger was alone at the open window with the supper on the table inside. He had the chance to put something in it."}
{"id":"t30.1","para":30,"tag":"HYPOTHESIS","subtype":"opportunity","confidence":"weak","profile":"typical","from":["t29.1","t20.1"],"reads":"Did the stable-boy, when he ran out with the dog, leave the door unlocked behind him?","text":"Watson's idea: the stranger doubled back and slipped in, or someone else did, while Hunter was out with the dog and the door stood open."}
{"id":"t31.1","para":31,"tag":"DROP","confidence":"strong","profile":"typical","from":["t30.1"],"reads":"The boy locked the door before he left it.","relations":[{"type":"drops","target":"t30.1"}],"text":"The door was locked and the window is too small for a man to get through. The stranger did not get in that evening."}
{"id":"t31.2","para":31,"tag":"GENRE","subtype":"watch-detective","confidence":"fair","profile":"careful","from":["t31.1","bk.genre_detective_signals"],"text":"Holmes wired specially about the door. Access to the stable is a key point for him: whoever took the horse later had to get through a locked door."}
{"id":"t32.1","para":32,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"he pulled on his large mackintosh and left the house","text":"Straker went out at about 1 a.m. in the rain to check the stables, wearing a large mackintosh."}
{"id":"t32.2","para":32,"tag":"AGENT","confidence":"weak","profile":"careful","from":["t32.1"],"reads":"although he does not seem to have quite realized its true significance","text":"He is said not to have grasped the significance, yet he was uneasy enough to go out alone at one in the rain without waking the lads. Mrs Straker reports only the reason he gave her."}
{"id":"t33.1","para":33,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"Hunter was sunk in a state of absolute stupor","text":"At 7 a.m.: the stable door is open, Hunter is stupefied, the stall is empty and Straker is missing."}
{"id":"t33.2","para":33,"tag":"QUESTION","subtype":"opportunity","confidence":"strong","profile":"typical","from":["t31.1","t33.1","bk.keyholder"],"reads":"The door was open","text":"Locked at nine and open at seven: who opened it?","options":["Someone with a duplicate or stolen key","Straker, who would have a key as master of the stable","Hunter, before the drug took hold"]}
{"id":"t34.1","para":34,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"They had heard nothing during the night, for they are both sound sleepers.","text":"The lads in the loft heard nothing."}
{"id":"t34.2","para":34,"tag":"ABSENCE","confidence":"weak","profile":"careful","from":["t29.1","t34.1","bk.guard_dog"],"text":"A guard dog is kept in the stable, yet nothing woke the lads overhead. Did the dog make no noise while the horse was taken? The account does not say.","hindsight":"A first-time reader must connect the dog of ¶29 with the silence of ¶34. They are five paragraphs apart and the dog is never mentioned again."}
{"id":"t35.1","para":35,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"John Straker’s overcoat was flapping from a furze-bush","text":"Straker's coat was hanging on a furze-bush a quarter-mile from the stables. His body lay in a hollow just beyond."}
{"id":"t35.2","para":35,"tag":"AGENT","confidence":"fair","profile":"careful","from":["t32.1","t35.1"],"text":"It was a rainy night, yet his coat is off and hung on a bush. Why would he take it off?","options":["It was torn off in the struggle","He threw it aside to fight","He took it off for some task","Someone else put it there"]}
{"id":"t35.3","para":35,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"there was a long, clean cut, inflicted evidently by some very sharp instrument","text":"Wounds: his head was smashed by a heavy weapon, and he has a long clean cut on the thigh from a very sharp blade."}
{"id":"t35.4","para":35,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"in his right hand he held a small knife, which was clotted with blood up to the handle","text":"Straker held a small, bloodied knife in his right hand."}
{"id":"t35.5","para":35,"tag":"NOTICE","confidence":"weak","profile":"careful","from":["t35.4","t32.1"],"text":"Straker went out to check on horses carrying a knife. What kind of knife, and why did he have it? It is described only as 'small'."}
{"id":"t35.6","para":35,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":["t21.1"],"reads":"in his left he clasped a red and black silk cravat","text":"In his left hand was a cravat that the maid identifies as the stranger's."}
{"id":"t36.1","para":36,"tag":"WEIGH","confidence":"strong","profile":"careful","from":["t29.2","t26.1"],"reads":"He was equally certain that the same stranger had, while standing at the window, drugged his curried mutton","text":"Hunter did not see the drugging. He concluded it. He was across the stable fetching the dog. It is a reasonable guess, but it is not observation."}
{"id":"t37.1","para":37,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":[],"reads":"there were abundant proofs in the mud which lay at the bottom of the fatal hollow that he had been there at the time of the struggle","text":"The horse was in the hollow where Straker died, at the time of the struggle."}
{"id":"t37.2","para":37,"tag":"TEST","confidence":"weak","profile":"careful","from":["t19.5","t14.2"],"reads":"all the gypsies of Dartmoor are on the alert","relations":[{"type":"strains","target":"t19.5"}],"text":"With a large reward and the gypsies themselves searching, a gypsy theft looks less likely. So does a horse that is merely loose. The horse is hidden well, or in a place nobody searches."}
{"id":"t37.3","para":37,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":["t20.2"],"reads":"contain an appreciable quantity of powdered opium","text":"Hunter's leftover curry contains powdered opium."}
{"id":"t37.4","para":37,"tag":"INFER","subtype":"opportunity","confidence":"strong","profile":"typical","from":["t37.3"],"reads":"the people at the house partook of the same dish on the same night without any ill effect","text":"The household ate the same dish unharmed, so the opium went into Hunter's portion only. It was added after his share was set apart: in the kitchen, on the path, at the window, or by Hunter himself."}
{"id":"t37.5","para":37,"tag":"DROP","confidence":"fair","profile":"typical","from":["t37.3","t33.2"],"relations":[{"type":"strains","target":"t33.2"}],"text":"The analysis confirms that Hunter really was drugged. That makes him an unlikely accomplice who opened the door."}
{"id":"t37.6","para":37,"tag":"PHYSICAL","confidence":"weak","profile":"expert","from":["t37.3","t20.2","bk.drug_taste"],"text":"Powdered opium has a taste of its own, and a curry would hide it. A stranger who met the maid by chance on the path could not have known the supper would be curry. Would a plain dish have given the drug away?","hindsight":"A first-time reader needs to know that opium has a noticeable taste, and to ask whether the poisoner chose the dish or was merely lucky. Few readers make this step unprompted."}
{"id":"t39.1","para":39,"tag":"GENRE","confidence":"fair","profile":"typical","from":["t12.1"],"reads":"Were he but gifted with imagination he might rise to great heights in his profession.","text":"Holmes calls Gregory competent but unimaginative. The 'natural' suspect the police chose may be the wrong one."}
{"id":"t39.2","para":39,"tag":"RECORD","profile":"typical","from":["t14.5","t19.3"],"reads":"he inhabited one of those villas which I have mentioned","text":"Simpson was lodging in the villas half a mile north and was found there easily."}
